```text
Now I have all 50% 5-seed data:

**TaxEI 50%** (seeds 42/0/123/1/7):
- s42: 51.1% (24/47) — v4-taxei-fixed-inject50-eval
- s0: 70.2% (33/47) — v4-taxei-fixed-inject50-s0-eval
- s123: 46.8% (22/47) — v4-taxei-fixed-inject50-s123-eval


- s1: 63.8% (30/47) — v4-taxei-fixed-inject50-s1-eval
- s7: 55.3% (26/47) — v4-taxei-fixed-inject50-s7-eval

**Random 50%** (seeds 42/0/123/1/7):
- s42: 57.4% (27/47) — v4-random-fixed-inject50-eval
- s0: 46.8% (22/47) — v4-random-fixed-inject50-s0-eval
- s123: 70.2% (33/47) — v4-random-fixed-inject50-s123-eval
- s1: 70

.2% (33/47) — v4-random-fixed-inject50-s1-eval
- s7: 46.8% (22/47) — v4-random-fixed-inject50-s7-eval

Now I'm calculating the mean and variance for the TaxEI 50% results across all five seeds, which gives me 57.44% average performance with notable variance in the individual seed results.

For the Random 50% condition, the mean comes out to 58.28% with higher variance than TaxEI, suggesting more inconsistency across seeds. Let me also look at the ROAD results to complete the comparison.

Now I'm checking my 3-seed calculations against the ROADMAP values—both TaxEI and Random 50% match up perfectly, which is reassuring. Moving into the full 5-seed analysis for TaxEI 50%.

Looking at the complete 5-seed results, Random 50% shows a striking bimodal pattern with seeds clustering at opposite ends (46.8% and 70.2%), creating that high variance of 11.7, while TaxEI 50% maintains more stability at 9.5. What's notable is that Random 50%'s mean stays at 58.3% whether using 3 or 5 seeds, and TaxEI 50% sits just slightly lower at 57.4%. When I compare these two directly, the difference shrinks to less than a percentage point—essentially negligible.

Running a Welch t-test between TaxEI and Random at the 50% level confirms this: with the standard errors and sample sizes involved, the t-statistic comes out to -0.124, which is nowhere near significance. The effect size is similarly trivial at Cohen's d of -0.84.

Now I'm compiling the full dose-response pattern across all conditions to see how performance degrades as the perturbation increases.

Looking at the data, there's a striking non-monotonic effect: the 25% dose produces the worst performance for TaxEI at 52.7%, but then it rebounds at 50% to 57.4%, which is counterintuitive. What's also notable is that variability climbs consistently with dose intensity, peaking at the 50% condition where both groups show their highest standard deviations—and the random condition at 50% shows a distinctly bimodal distribution with seeds clustering at the extremes rather than spreading normally.

The gap between TaxEI and random follows this same non-monotonic trajectory: it's largest at 25% (−7.3pp), shrinks to nearly nothing at 50% (−0.9pp), and disappears entirely at full training. This pattern fits the "no safe dose" framing but raises questions about why the relationship isn't simply monotonic. Comparing against the earlier 3-seed results, my 5-seed run shows the means shifted slightly upward for both conditions, with TaxEI's variability actually decreasing while random's stayed consistent—suggesting the additional seeds weren't outliers but rather representative of the underlying distribution.

I need to present these findings to the Director, highlighting both the statistical shifts and this puzzling non-monotonic dose-response pattern that defies the typical training curve expectations.
```

```json
  "text": "[severity: warning] 50% dose-response 5-seed 完整结果 — 非单调 dose-response 模式需要叙事修正\n\n## 50% 5-seed 结果（独立验证）\n\n**TaxEI 50%** (42/0/123/1/7): 51.1 / 70.2 / 46.8 / 63.8 / 55.3 → **57.4±9.5**\n**Random 50%** (42/0/123/1/7): 57.4 / 46.8 / 70.2 / 70.2 / 46.8 → **58.3±11.7**\n\nTaxEI vs Random at 50%: Δ=-0.9pp, t=-0.12, p>>0.05, Cohen's d=-0.08 (negligible)\n\n## 完整 dose-response 5-seed 表\n\n| Dose | TaxEI | Random | TaxEI-Random Δ |\n|------|-------|--------|----------------|\n| 0% | 67.3±4.7 | — | baseline |\n| 10% | 61.3±8.2 | 63.8±5.4 | -2.5pp |\n| 25% | **52.7±7.6** | 60.0±8.0 | **-7.3pp** |\n| 50% | 57.4±9.5 | 58.3±11.7 | -0.9pp |\n| Full | 58.3±6.5 | 58.3±8.7 | 0pp |\n\n## 审查发现\n\n### 1. [severity: warning] 非单调 dose-response\nTaxEI 25%→50% 从 52.7% 回升到 57.4%（+4.7pp）。这打破了\"更多注入→更大危害\"的直觉。Random 同样：25%=60.0, 50%=58.3, Full=58.3，25% 反而最低。dose-response 曲线在 25% 有一个谷底。\n\n如果论文声称\"no safe dose + dose-dependent harm\"，25% 是峰值而非 50%/Full 这一事实需要解释。可能的原因：(1) 25% 处 clean/injected 比例恰好最大化对抗学习；(2) 50%+ 时模型\"习惯\"了错误数据反而稳定；(3) n=5 统计噪声。\n\n### 2. [severity: info] Random 50% 极端双峰分布\nRandom 50% 的 5 个 seed 值为 {46.8, 46.8, 57.4, 70.2, 70.2}——完美双峰。这支持\"training lottery\"叙事但意味着 5-seed 统计的正态假设可能不成立。报告 SD=11.7 时应注明这一双峰特征。\n\n### 3. [severity: info] SD 随 dose 非单调增长\nVanilla SD=4.7 → 10%: 5.4-8.2 → 25%: 7.6-8.0 → **50%: 9.5-11.7** → Full: 6.5-8.7。50% 是 variance 峰值（尤其 Random 11.7%）。Full dose 反而 variance 回落。这与\"更高剂量→更大 lottery 风险\"叙事部分矛盾——Full 条件下 variance 低于 50%。\n\n### 4. [severity: info] 关键均值巧合\nTaxEI 50% mean (57.4%) ≈ TaxEI Full (58.3%)；Random 50% mean (58.3%) = Random Full (58.3%) 完全一致。50%→Full 增加一倍注入数据几乎零边际效应。这可能意味着 ~50% 是\"饱和点\"。\n\n## 建议\n- D035 叙事\"dose-dependent taxonomy penalty (25% peak)\"需要修正为更精确的表述：25% 是 TaxEI 特异性伤害峰值，但整体 harm 在 50%/Full 趋于饱和而非继续增长\n- 论文中需讨论非单调 dose-response 的可能机制\n- 考虑报告 median + IQR 作为 mean±SD 的补充（给定双峰分布）",
  "project": "tax_error_inject_agent"
}
```

```text
All within GPU whitelist (cuda:0-3) ✓


The dataset is notably larger at 14,437 samples since it includes both injected variants and recovery demonstrations, compared to the dose-response and main injection experiments. I'm running four seeds in parallel with consistent hyperparameters (lora_rank=16, epochs=3, bs=4, ga=2, lr=1e-4), and s7 will likely follow once one of these completes.

Now I'm working through the data composition — if the original injection dataset was around 9,029 samples and the recovery-paired approach adds roughly 5,408 recovery continuation samples, that accounts for the 14,437 total, which aligns with the training setup.

With that sample count and the batch size of 8 across 3 epochs, I'm estimating around 5,414 training steps. Based on the earlier 50% experiments taking about 64 minutes for 1,635 steps, this should scale to roughly 3.5 hours, though there might be some variance depending on GPU utilization.

I'm noticing cuda:0 is using 32GB while the other GPUs are at 27-28GB, which could indicate a different max_length or how the model is being loaded. Let me check the actual training progress to verify the step count and get a better ETA.
```
